<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the generalized Cartan–Hadamard isoperimetric conjecture in every dimension. In a complete simply connected smooth manifold with sectional curvature at most <i>κ</i> ≤ 0, every finite-volume set of finite ambient perimeter has perimeter at least that of the equal-volume ball in curvature <i>κ</i>. For bounded positive-volume sets, equality in the Euclidean comparison holds precisely when the set agrees up to null sets with an open region isometric, with its induced metric, to a round Euclidean ball.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $n\ge2$, let $\kappa\le0$, and let $(M^n,g)$ be a complete simply connected smooth Riemannian manifold with $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_g\le\kappa$. Every measurable set $E\subset M$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(E)<\infty$ and $P_g(E)<\infty$ satisfies $$P_g(E)\ge \mathcal I_{\sqrt{-\kappa}}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(E)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Bounds full ambient perimeter below by the equal-volume model ball perimeter, including unbounded finite-volume sets.
- **C2** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-equality)): Let $(M^n,g)$ be complete, simply connected and smooth, with $n\ge2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_g\le0$. Let $E\subset M$ be bounded and measurable, with $0<\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(E)<\infty$ and $P_g(E)<\infty$. Then $$P_g(E)=n\omega_n^{1/n}\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(E)^{(n-1)/n}$$ if and only if $E$ agrees up to ambient volume zero with an open region whose induced Riemannian metric is isometric to a round Euclidean ball.
  - Explanation: Classifies bounded positive-volume Euclidean equality sets by the induced metric on the entire occupied region.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-model-ball-spectral)): Let $n\ge2$, $\kappa\le0$ and $1<p<\infty$. Let $(M^n,g)$ be complete, simply connected, smooth and without boundary, with $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_g\le\kappa$. For a nonempty smooth domain $D\Subset M$, let $B_\kappa(\|D\|)$ be a geodesic ball of volume $\|D\|=\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g(D)$ in the simply connected $n$-dimensional space form of curvature $\kappa$. Then $$\lambda_{1,p}(D)\ge\lambda_{1,p}\bigl(B_\kappa(\|D\|)\bigr), \qquad T_p(D)\le T_p\bigl(B_\kappa(\|D\|)\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Compares the Dirichlet variational value and quotient-normalized torsional rigidity with those of an equal-volume model ball.
- **C4** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-maps)): The map $\Phi_{p,a}$ and the positive function $f_{p,a}$ are smooth on $M$. For every $X\in T_xM$, $$\label{eq:map-comparison} \begin{aligned} \|d\Phi_{p,a}(X)\|&\le f_{p,a}\|X\|,\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}u_{p,a}&\le du_{p,a}\otimes du_{p,a} -\frac12\bigl(b^2+f_{p,a}^2+\|\nabla u_{p,a}\|^2\bigr)g. \end{aligned}$$ The second inequality is one of quadratic forms. The functions depend smoothly on $(p,a,x)$. For fixed $o\in M$, parallel transport of the first component of $\Phi_{p,a}$ from $p$ to $o$ along the unique geodesic also depends smoothly on $(p,a,x)$.
  - Explanation: Controls the differential of a sphere-valued comparison map and the Hessian of its logarithmic weight.
- **C5** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-distance-comparison)): Let $(M^n,g)$ be complete and simply connected with $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_g\le0$. For $p\in M$, write $\log_p=\exp_p^{-1}$, $r_p(x)=d(p,x)$, and $D_p=r_p^2/2$. Then $$\label{eq:distance-metric-comparison} \mathop{\mathrm{Hess}}_gD_p\ge g,\qquad (\exp_p)^*g\ge g_p, \qquad \|d\log_p(X)\|_{g_p}\le \|X\|_g.$$ Here $g_p$ on $T_pM$ denotes its constant Euclidean metric.
  - Explanation: Gives convexity of squared distance, metric expansion by the exponential map, and contraction by its inverse.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-balancing)): Let $\mu$ be a compactly supported Borel probability measure on $M$ with $\mu(\{x\})=0$ for every $x$. If $\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mu\subset B_D(o)$, there are $p\in B_D(o)$ and finite $a>0$ such that $$\label{eq:balanced-map} \int_M\Phi_{p,a}(x)\,d\mu(x)=0\quad\text{in }T_pM\oplus\mathbb R.$$
  - Explanation: Provides center and scale parameters balancing a compactly supported nonatomic probability measure.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-extrinsic-sobolev)): For every Lipschitz function $v$ with compact support in $\Sigma^\circ$, $$\label{eq:extrinsic-sobolev} Q_\Sigma(v)\ge Y_m\left(\int_\Sigma \|v\|^q\,d\sigma\right)^{2/q}.$$ The inequality also holds for the zero extension of every $v\in H^1_0(U)$, where $U\Subset\Sigma^\circ$ is open.
  - Explanation: Bounds hypersurface energy below by the sharp critical norm under the surrounding curvature and dimension assumptions.
- **C8** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-square-estimate)): Fix $p\in M$ and $a>0$, and let $\Phi=\Phi_{p,a}$ and $f=e^u=f_{p,a}$ be the map and functions of Lemma <a href="#lem:comparison-maps" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:comparison-maps">4</a>. For every compactly supported Lipschitz function $v$ in $\Sigma^\circ$, $$\label{eq:square-estimate} \begin{aligned} Q_\Sigma(v) &\ge\int_\Sigma\left( mf^2v^2+m(h+Nu)^2v^2 +c\|\nabla_\Sigma v-\alpha v\nabla_\Sigma u\|^2\right)\,d\sigma\\ &\ge\int_\Sigma\left( v^2\|d(\Phi\|_\Sigma)\|^2 +c\|\nabla_\Sigma v-\alpha v\nabla_\Sigma u\|^2\right)\,d\sigma. \end{aligned}$$ The same inequalities hold on $H^1_0(U)$ for every $U\Subset\Sigma^\circ$. In particular, $Q_\Sigma$ is nonnegative on all these functions.
  - Explanation: Completes the hypersurface energy into nonnegative squares and a comparison-map energy.
- **C9** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-local-sobolev-threshold)): For every open $U\Subset\Sigma^\circ$ and $\epsilon>0$, there is $C_{\epsilon,U}<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:local-sobolev-threshold} (Y_m-\epsilon)\\|v\\|_q^2 \le c\int_\Sigma\|\nabla_\Sigma v\|^2\,d\sigma +C_{\epsilon,U}\int_\Sigma v^2\,d\sigma, \qquad v\in H^1_0(U).$$ The inclusion $H^1_0(U)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21AA> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}L^2(U)$ is compact.
  - Explanation: Establishes local critical norm control and compactness in the lower norm without boundary regularity.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-subthreshold-minimizer)): For nonempty open $U\Subset\Sigma^\circ$, put $$Y(U)=\inf\{Q_\Sigma(v):v\in H^1_0(U),\ \\|v\\|_q=1\}.$$ If $Y(U)<Y_m$, the infimum is attained.
  - Explanation: Establishes attainment of a critical quotient strictly below the Euclidean threshold.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bv-compactness)): Let $K$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary. If functions $u_j$ vanish outside $K$ almost everywhere and $$\sup_j\bigl(\\|u_j\\|_{L^1}+\|Du_j\|(M)\bigr)<\infty,$$ then a subsequence converges in $L^1(M)$. Total variation is lower semicontinuous under this convergence. If the $u_j$ are characteristic functions, their limit is a characteristic function.
  - Explanation: Provides compactness and lower semicontinuity for bounded-variation functions confined to compact support.
- **C12** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bv-flow)): Let $F:M\to M$ be an orientation-preserving smooth diffeomorphism, and let $E$ have finite perimeter. If the right side below is finite, then $$\label{eq:bv-flow-jacobian} P(FE)=\int_{\partial^*E} J_{n-1}\bigl(dF_x\|_{N_E(x)^\perp}\bigr)\,d\mathcal H_g^{n-1}(x).$$ All Jacobians and adjoints use the source and target Riemannian metrics. For a smooth flow $F_t$ with velocity $Z$ and a set whose perimeter support is compact, $$\label{eq:bv-flow-first-variation} \left.\frac d{dt}\right\|_{t=0}P(F_tE) =\int_{\partial^*E}\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{N_E^\perp}Z\,d\mathcal H_g^{n-1}.$$ For a fixed finite family of flows and perimeter supports contained in a fixed compact set, there are common $C,\tau>0$ such that $\|P(F_tE)-P(E)\|\le C\|t\|P(E)$ for $\|t\|<\tau$.
  - Explanation: Transforms full ambient perimeter by tangential Jacobians and identifies its variation under smooth flows.
- **C13** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-confined-regularity)): For each $v\in(0,\|B\|)$ there is a minimizer $E$ in <a href="#eq:confined-profile" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:confined-profile">[eq:confined-profile]</a>. It has a regular representative for which $\Gamma=\partial E$ is the support of its perimeter measure and is contained in $\overline B$. There is a compact set $S\subset B$ such that* 1. *$\dim_{\mathcal H}S\le n-8$, with $S=\varnothing$ if $n<8$;* 2. *$\Sigma=\Gamma\setminus S$ is a boundaryless $C^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, smooth on $\Sigma\cap B$;* 3. *$\Gamma$ is $C^{1,1}$ in an ambient neighborhood of $\partial B$, and $S$ is disjoint from a collar of $\partial B$;* 4. *with $d\sigma$ denoting area on $\Sigma$, for every Borel set $A\subset M$ one has $$\label{eq:perimeter-area} \|D\chi_E\|(A)=\int_{A\cap\Sigma}d\sigma, \qquad P(E)=\int_\Sigma d\sigma=I(v).$$* *The unit normal on $\Sigma$ is the outward normal of $E$.
  - Explanation: Provides confined perimeter minimizers with regular and singular boundary portions, including contact perimeter.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-lipschitz)): The profile $I$ is positive and locally Lipschitz on $(0,\|B\|)$.
  - Explanation: Establishes positivity and local Lipschitz continuity of the confined profile.
- **C15** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-curvature)): Let $v\in(0,\|B\|)$ be a differentiability point of $I$, and let $E$ be a minimizer as in Proposition <a href="#prop:confined-regularity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:confined-regularity">13</a>. With $mh=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_\Sigma N$ and $h_0=I'(v)/m$, one has $$I'(v)>0,\qquad h=h_0\ \text{on }\Sigma\cap B, \qquad 0\le h\le h_0\quad\text{almost everywhere on }\Sigma.$$ In particular, $h^2\le h_0^2$ almost everywhere on $\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Identifies free-boundary mean curvature and bounds contact curvature using the profile derivative.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-bv-deletion)): Let $E$ have finite volume and perimeter in a complete smooth Riemannian manifold, let $x\in M$, and let $0<r<\operatorname{inj}(x)$. Then $$\label{eq:bv-deletion} P(E\setminus B_r(x))\le P(E;M\setminus\overline B_r(x)) +\mathcal H_g^{n-1}(\partial B_r(x)).$$ For all but countably many radii in any bounded interval, $P(E;\partial B_r(x))=0$, so the perimeters on the two open sides sum to $P(E)$.
  - Explanation: Bounds perimeter after deletion of a geodesic ball by remaining perimeter and sphere area.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-area-growth)): Let $0<v<\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(B)$, and let $E$ be a confined minimizing region of volume $v$. There are constants $C<\infty$ and $r_0>0$, depending on $E$ and $B$, such that $$\label{eq:singular-area-growth} \sigma\bigl(\Sigma\cap B_r(x)\bigr)\le Cr^m \qquad(x\in S,\ 0<r<r_0).$$
  - Explanation: Controls boundary area growth near singular points by deletion and volume repair.
- **C18** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-capacity-cutoffs)): Let $\Gamma$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold, and let $S\subset\Gamma$ be closed. Suppose that $\Sigma=\Gamma\setminus S$ is an embedded, boundaryless, locally $C^{1,1}$ hypersurface of dimension $m\ge3$ and finite area. Assume $\mathcal H^{m-2}(S)=0$ and that, for some $C,r_0>0$, $$\sigma(\Sigma\cap B_r(x))\le Cr^m \qquad(x\in S,\ 0<r<r_0).$$ There are $\eta_j\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}_c(\Sigma)$ such that $$\label{eq:singular-cutoff-properties} 0\le\eta_j\le1,\qquad \eta_j(x)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}1\quad(x\in\Sigma), \qquad \int_\Sigma\|\nabla_\Sigma\eta_j\|^2\,d\sigma\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}0.$$ If $S=\varnothing$, the choice $\eta_j=1$ works in every dimension.
  - Explanation: Provides compactly supported cutoffs approaching the constant function with vanishing gradient energy.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-constant-test)): Let $v\in(0,\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(B))$ be a differentiability point of $I$, and let $E$ minimize the confined perimeter at volume $v$. Then $$\label{eq:constant-sobolev} m\int_\Sigma(h^2-b^2)\,d\sigma \ge Y_m I(v)^{(m-2)/m}.$$
  - Explanation: Justifies the constant hypersurface test on confined minimizers despite singular boundary points.
- **C20** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-profile-ode)): For almost every $v\in(0,\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(B))$, $$\label{eq:profile-differential} I'(v)\ge m\sqrt{b^2+\left(\frac{s_m}{I(v)}\right)^{2/m}}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the confined-profile derivative below by the model differential expression.
- **C21** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-normalized-comparison)): Let $n\ge4$ and $b\in\{0,1\}$. If $M^n$ is complete, simply connected and smooth, with $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_M\le-b^2$, then every bounded measurable set $E\subset M$ of finite perimeter and positive volume satisfies $$P(E)\ge\mathcal I_b(\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}(E)).$$
  - Explanation: Compares bounded measurable finite-perimeter sets with model balls in the normalized curvature regimes.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-smooth-bv-approximation)): Let $M$ be a complete connected smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary. Let $E$ have finite ambient perimeter, and suppose that $\|E\setminus K\|=0$ for a compact set $K\subset M$. For every open neighborhood $U\supset K$ with compact closure, there are relatively compact open sets $E_j\Subset U$ with smooth boundary such that $$\|E_j\mathbin{\triangle}E\|\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}0, \qquad P(E_j)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}P(E).$$ The sets $E_j$ need not be connected.
  - Explanation: Approximates compactly supported finite-perimeter sets by smooth domains with converging volume and perimeter.
- **C23** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-volume-cutoff)): Let $M$ be a complete connected smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary. Let $J:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be continuous and increasing, with $J(0)=0$. Suppose that $P(F)\ge J(\|F\|)$ for every bounded finite-perimeter set $F\subset M$. Then the same inequality holds for every measurable $E$ of finite volume and finite ambient perimeter.
  - Explanation: Extends a continuous increasing perimeter bound from bounded sets to all finite-volume finite-perimeter sets.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-equality-boundary)): Let $M^n$ be complete, simply connected and smooth, with $n\ge2$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{sec}}_M\le0$. Suppose that a bounded measurable set $E\subset M$ has positive volume $V$, finite ambient perimeter $A$, and $$A=n\omega_n^{1/n}V^{(n-1)/n}.$$ Put $m=n-1$, $R=(V/\omega_n)^{1/n}$, and $H_0=m/R$. The support $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\|D\chi_E\|$ is compact and has a decomposition $\Gamma=\Sigma\mathbin{\dot\cup}Z$ with the following properties:* 1. *$\Sigma$ is a relatively open, boundaryless, embedded $C^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, and $Z$ is compact, with $\dim_{\mathcal H}Z\le n-8$. In particular, $Z=\varnothing$ when $n<8$.* 2. *The side occupied by $E$ defines an outward unit normal $N$ on $\Sigma$, and $$\label{eq:equality-perimeter-area} \|D\chi_E\|=d\sigma\lfloor\Sigma, \qquad \sigma(\Sigma)=A=s_mR^m, \qquad \overline\Sigma=\Gamma.$$ Here $d\sigma$ denotes induced area on $\Sigma$, extended by zero to $M$.* 3. *There are $C<\infty$ and $r_0>0$ such that $$\label{eq:equality-area-growth} \sigma\bigl(\Sigma\cap B_r(x)\bigr)\le Cr^m \qquad(x\in\Gamma,\ 0<r<r_0).$$* 4. *The outward trace mean curvature satisfies $H=\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_{T\Sigma}N=H_0$ almost everywhere on $\Sigma$.* *These assertions concern the perimeter support and the measurable class of $E$, not the topological boundary of an arbitrary representative.
  - Explanation: Describes regularity, area growth, and constant outward curvature of the perimeter support of an equality set.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-equality-graph-regularity)): Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^m$ be open, with $m\ge1$, and let $u\in C^{1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)$ for $0<\alpha<1$. Let $G(y,z)$ be the matrix of a smooth Riemannian metric on a neighborhood of the graph of $u$ in $\mathbb R^m\times\mathbb R$, and put $$W(y,z)=\sqrt{\det G(y,z)},\qquad L_g(y,z,p)=W(y,z) \left[(-p,1)^TG(y,z)^{-1}(-p,1)\right]^{1/2}.$$ Fix $H_0>0$ and write $\mathcal B(y,z,p)=\partial_zL_g(y,z,p)-H_0W(y,z)$. If $$\int_\Omega\left[ \partial_pL_g(y,u,Du)\cdot D\zeta +\mathcal B(y,u,Du)\zeta\right]dy=0 \qquad(\zeta\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)),$$ then $u\in C^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega)$.
  - Explanation: Upgrades a stationary graph from Hölder gradient regularity to a locally Lipschitz gradient.
- **C26** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-equality-cutoffs)): Under the hypotheses of [prop:equality-boundary](#prop:equality-boundary), there are $\eta_j\in\mathop{\mathrm{Lip}}_c(\Sigma)$ such that $$0\le\eta_j\le1, \qquad \eta_j(x)\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}1\quad(x\in\Sigma), \qquad \int_\Sigma\|\nabla_\Sigma\eta_j\|^2\,d\sigma\mathrel{\begingroup \let\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\endcsname \let\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\endcsname \let\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}% PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\endcsname\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\endgroup}0.$$
  - Explanation: Provides vanishing-energy cutoffs on the regular boundary of an equality set.

Then $$\\label{eq:distance-metric-comparison}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hess}}_gD_p\\ge g,\\qquad (\\exp_p)^*g\\ge g_p,\n \\qquad |d\\log_p(X)|_{g_p}\\le |X|_g.$$ Here $g_p$ on $T_pM$ denotes its constant Euclidean metric.",
      "explanation": "Gives convexity of squared distance, metric expansion by the exponential map, and contraction by its inverse.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

        }

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-balancing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a compactly supported Borel probability measure on $M$ with $\\mu(\\{x\\})=0$ for every $x$.         "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $p\\in M$ and $a>0$, and let $\\Phi=\\Phi_{p,a}$ and $f=e^u=f_{p,a}$ be the map and functions of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:comparison-maps\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:comparison-maps\">4</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-sobolev-threshold",
        "exact_quote": "For every open $U\\Subset\\Sigma^\\circ$ and $\\epsilon>0$, there is $C_{\\epsilon,U}<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:local-sobolev-threshold}\n (Y_m-\\epsilon)\\|v\\|_q^2\n \\le c\\int_\\Sigma|\\nabla_\\Sigma v|^2\\,d\\sigma\n       +C_{\\epsilon,U}\\int_\\Sigma v^2\\,d\\sigma,\n \\qquad v\\in H^1_0(U).$$ The inclusion $H^1_0(U)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF21AA> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}L^2(U)$ is compact.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-local-sobolev-threshold",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary. If functions $u_j$ vanish outside $K$ almost everywhere and $$\\sup_j\\bigl(\\|u_j\\|_{L^1}+|Du_j|(M)\\bigr)<\\infty,$$ then a subsequence converges in $L^1(M)$. Total variation is lower semicontinuous under this convergence. If the $u_j$ are characteristic functions, their limit is a characteristic function.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary.           "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-flow",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F:M\\to M$ be an orientation-preserving smooth diffeomorphism, and let $E$ have finite perimeter. If the right side below is finite, then $$\\label{eq:bv-flow-jacobian}\n P(FE)=\\int_{\\partial^*E}\n J_{n-1}\\bigl(dF_x|_{N_E(x)^\\perp}\\bigr)\\,d\\mathcal H_g^{n-1}(x).$$ All Jacobians and adjoints use the source and target Riemannian metrics. For a smooth flow $F_t$ with velocity $Z$ and a set whose perimeter support is compact, $$\\label{eq:bv-flow-first-variation}\n \\left.\\frac d{dt}\\right|_{t=0}P(F_tE)\n =\\int_{\\partial^*E}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{N_E^\\perp}Z\\,d\\mathcal H_g^{n-1}.$$ For a fixed finite family of flows and perimeter supports contained in a fixed compact set, there are common $C,\\tau>0$ such that $|P(F_tE)-P(E)|\\le C|t|P(E)$ for $|t|<\\tau$.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-flow",

          "source_id": "PM-337.1"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-flow",
          "exact_quote": "Let $F:M\\to M$ be an orientation-preserving smooth diffeomorphism, and let $E$ have finite perimeter. If the right side below is finite, then $$\\label{eq:bv-flow-jacobian}\n P(FE)=\\int_{\\partial^*E}\n J_{n-1}\\bigl(dF_x|_{N_E(x)^\\perp}\\bigr)\\,d\\mathcal H_g^{n-1}(x).$$ All Jacobians and adjoints use the source and target Riemannian metrics. For a smooth flow $F_t$ with velocity $Z$ and a set whose perimeter support is compact, $$\\label{eq:bv-flow-first-variation}\n \\left.\\frac d{dt}\\right|_{t=0}P(F_tE)\n =\\int_{\\partial^*E}\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{N_E^\\perp}Z\\,d\\mathcal H_g^{n-1}.$$ For a fixed finite family of flows and perimeter supports contained in a fixed compact set, there are common $C,\\tau>0$ such that $|P(F_tE)-P(E)|\\le C|t|P(E)$ for $|t|<\\tau$.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-flow"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-confined-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "For each $v\\in(0,|B|)$ there is a minimizer $E$ in <a href=\"#eq:confined-profile\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:confined-profile\">[eq:confined-profile]</a>. It has a regular representative for which $\\Gamma=\\partial E$ is the support of its perimeter measure and is contained in $\\overline B$. There is a compact set $S\\subset B$ such that*\n\n1.  *$\\dim_{\\mathcal H}S\\le n-8$, with $S=\\varnothing$ if $n<8$;*\n\n2.  *$\\Sigma=\\Gamma\\setminus S$ is a boundaryless $C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, smooth on $\\Sigma\\cap B$;*\n\n3.  *$\\Gamma$ is $C^{1,1}$ in an ambient neighborhood of $\\partial B$, and $S$ is disjoint from a collar of $\\partial B$;*\n\n\n4.  *with $d\\sigma$ denoting area on $\\Sigma$, for every Borel set $A\\subset M$ one has $$\\label{eq:perimeter-area}\n      |D\\chi_E|(A)=\\int_{A\\cap\\Sigma}d\\sigma,\n      \\qquad P(E)=\\int_\\Sigma d\\sigma=I(v).$$*\n\n*The unit normal on $\\Sigma$ is the outward normal of $E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $v\\in(0,|B|)$ be a differentiability point of $I$, and let $E$ be a minimizer as in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:confined-regularity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:confined-regularity\">13</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-bv-deletion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $E$ have finite volume and perimeter in a complete smooth Riemannian manifold, let $x\\in M$, and let $0<r<\\operatorname{inj}(x)$.         "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-capacity-cutoffs",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold, and let $S\\subset\\Gamma$ be closed. Suppose that $\\Sigma=\\Gamma\\setminus S$ is an embedded, boundaryless, locally $C^{1,1}$ hypersurface of dimension $m\\ge3$ and finite area. Assume $\\mathcal H^{m-2}(S)=0$ and that, for some $C,r_0>0$, $$\\sigma(\\Sigma\\cap B_r(x))\\le Cr^m\n \\qquad(x\\in S,\\ 0<r<r_0).$$ There are $\\eta_j\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Lip}}_c(\\Sigma)$ such that $$\\label{eq:singular-cutoff-properties}\n 0\\le\\eta_j\\le1,\\qquad \\eta_j(x)\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}1\\quad(x\\in\\Sigma),\n \\qquad \\int_\\Sigma|\\nabla_\\Sigma\\eta_j|^2\\,d\\sigma\\mathrel{\\begingroup\n  \\let\\hookrightarrowP PaperOriginalhookrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\let\\mapstoP PaperOriginalmapsto\\endcsname\n  \\let\\longrightarrowP PaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27F6> >> BDC}%\n  PPaperOriginallongrightarrow\\endcsname\\pdfliteral direct{EMC}\\endgroup}0.$$ If $S=\\varnothing$, the choice $\\eta_j=1$ works in every dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-capacity-cutoffs",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold, and let $S\\subset\\Gamma$ be closed.           "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Gamma$ be a compact subset of a smooth Riemannian manifold, and let $S\\subset\\Gamma$ be closed. Suppose that $\\Sigma=\\Gamma\\setminus S$ is an embedded, boundaryless, locally $C^{1,1}$ hypersurface of dimension $m\\ge3$ and finite area.           "exact_quote": "Let $v\\in(0,\\mathop{\\mathrm{Vol}}(B))$ be a differentiability point of $I$, and let $E$ minimize the confined perimeter at volume $v$.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a complete connected smooth Riemannian manifold without boundary. Let $J:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be continuous and increasing, with $J(0)=0$.       },
      "evidence": [

        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-equality-boundary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M^n$ be complete, simply connected and smooth, with $n\\ge2$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{sec}}_M\\le0$. Suppose that a bounded measurable set $E\\subset M$ has positive volume $V$, finite ambient perimeter $A$, and $$A=n\\omega_n^{1/n}V^{(n-1)/n}.$$ Put $m=n-1$, $R=(V/\\omega_n)^{1/n}$, and $H_0=m/R$. The support $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_E|$ is compact and has a decomposition $\\Gamma=\\Sigma\\mathbin{\\dot\\cup}Z$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma$ is a relatively open, boundaryless, embedded $C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, and $Z$ is compact, with $\\dim_{\\mathcal H}Z\\le n-8$. In particular, $Z=\\varnothing$ when $n<8$.*\n\n2.  *The side occupied by $E$ defines an outward unit normal $N$ on $\\Sigma$, and $$\\label{eq:equality-perimeter-area}\n     |D\\chi_E|=d\\sigma\\lfloor\\Sigma,\n     \\qquad \\sigma(\\Sigma)=A=s_mR^m,\n     \\qquad \\overline\\Sigma=\\Gamma.$$ Here $d\\sigma$ denotes induced area on $\\Sigma$, extended by zero to $M$.*\n\n3.  *There are $C<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:equality-area-growth}\n     \\sigma\\bigl(\\Sigma\\cap B_r(x)\\bigr)\\le Cr^m\n     \\qquad(x\\in\\Gamma,\\ 0<r<r_0).$$*\n\n4.  *The outward trace mean curvature satisfies $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{T\\Sigma}N=H_0$ almost everywhere on $\\Sigma$.*\n\n*These assertions concern the perimeter support and the measurable class of $E$, not the topological boundary of an arbitrary representative.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-equality-boundary",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M^n$ be complete, simply connected and smooth, with $n\\ge2$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{sec}}_M\\le0$. Suppose that a bounded measurable set $E\\subset M$ has positive volume $V$, finite ambient perimeter $A$, and $$A=n\\omega_n^{1/n}V^{(n-1)/n}.$$ Put $m=n-1$, $R=(V/\\omega_n)^{1/n}$, and $H_0=m/R$. The support $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_E|$ is compact and has a decomposition $\\Gamma=\\Sigma\\mathbin{\\dot\\cup}Z$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma$ is a relatively open, boundaryless, embedded $C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, and $Z$ is compact, with $\\dim_{\\mathcal H}Z\\le n-8$. In particular, $Z=\\varnothing$ when $n<8$.*\n\n2.  *The side occupied by $E$ defines an outward unit normal $N$ on $\\Sigma$, and $$\\label{eq:equality-perimeter-area}\n     |D\\chi_E|=d\\sigma\\lfloor\\Sigma,\n     \\qquad \\sigma(\\Sigma)=A=s_mR^m,\n     \\qquad \\overline\\Sigma=\\Gamma.$$ Here $d\\sigma$ denotes induced area on $\\Sigma$, extended by zero to $M$.*\n\n3.  *There are $C<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:equality-area-growth}\n     \\sigma\\bigl(\\Sigma\\cap B_r(x)\\bigr)\\le Cr^m\n     \\qquad(x\\in\\Gamma,\\ 0<r<r_0).$$*\n\n4.  *The outward trace mean curvature satisfies $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{T\\Sigma}N=H_0$ almost everywhere on $\\Sigma$.*\n\n*These assertions concern the perimeter support and the measurable class of $E$, not the topological boundary of an arbitrary representative.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M^n$ be complete, simply connected and smooth, with $n\\ge2$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{sec}}_M\\le0$. The support $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_E|$ is compact and has a decomposition $\\Gamma=\\Sigma\\mathbin{\\dot\\cup}Z$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma$ is a relatively open, boundaryless, embedded $C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, and $Z$ is compact, with $\\dim_{\\mathcal H}Z\\le n-8$. In particular, $Z=\\varnothing$ when $n<8$.*\n\n2.  *The side occupied by $E$ defines an outward unit normal $N$ on $\\Sigma$, and $$\\label{eq:equality-perimeter-area}\n     |D\\chi_E|=d\\sigma\\lfloor\\Sigma,\n     \\qquad \\sigma(\\Sigma)=A=s_mR^m,\n     \\qquad \\overline\\Sigma=\\Gamma.$$ Here $d\\sigma$ denotes induced area on $\\Sigma$, extended by zero to $M$.*\n\n3.  *There are $C<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:equality-area-growth}\n     \\sigma\\bigl(\\Sigma\\cap B_r(x)\\bigr)\\le Cr^m\n     \\qquad(x\\in\\Gamma,\\ 0<r<r_0).$$*\n\n4.  *The outward trace mean curvature satisfies $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{T\\Sigma}N=H_0$ almost everywhere on $\\Sigma$.*\n\n*These assertions concern the perimeter support and the measurable class of $E$, not the topological boundary of an arbitrary representative.",
      "explanation": "Describes regularity, area growth, and constant outward curvature of the perimeter support of an equality set.",

      "hypotheses": [

The support $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}|D\\chi_E|$ is compact and has a decomposition $\\Gamma=\\Sigma\\mathbin{\\dot\\cup}Z$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$\\Sigma$ is a relatively open, boundaryless, embedded $C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ hypersurface, and $Z$ is compact, with $\\dim_{\\mathcal H}Z\\le n-8$. In particular, $Z=\\varnothing$ when $n<8$.*\n\n2.  *The side occupied by $E$ defines an outward unit normal $N$ on $\\Sigma$, and $$\\label{eq:equality-perimeter-area}\n     |D\\chi_E|=d\\sigma\\lfloor\\Sigma,\n     \\qquad \\sigma(\\Sigma)=A=s_mR^m,\n     \\qquad \\overline\\Sigma=\\Gamma.$$ Here $d\\sigma$ denotes induced area on $\\Sigma$, extended by zero to $M$.*\n\n3.  *There are $C<\\infty$ and $r_0>0$ such that $$\\label{eq:equality-area-growth}\n     \\sigma\\bigl(\\Sigma\\cap B_r(x)\\bigr)\\le Cr^m\n     \\qquad(x\\in\\Gamma,\\ 0<r<r_0).$$*\n\n4.  *The outward trace mean curvature satisfies $H=\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_{T\\Sigma}N=H_0$ almost everywhere on $\\Sigma$.*\n\n*These assertions concern the perimeter support and the measurable class of $E$, not the topological boundary of an arbitrary representative.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-equality-graph-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be open, with $m\\ge1$, and let $u\\in C^{1,\\alpha}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ for $0<\\alpha<1$. Let $G(y,z)$ be the matrix of a smooth Riemannian metric on a neighborhood of the graph of $u$ in $\\mathbb R^m\\times\\mathbb R$, and put $$W(y,z)=\\sqrt{\\det G(y,z)},\\qquad\n L_g(y,z,p)=W(y,z)\n \\left[(-p,1)^TG(y,z)^{-1}(-p,1)\\right]^{1/2}.$$ Fix $H_0>0$ and write $\\mathcal B(y,z,p)=\\partial_zL_g(y,z,p)-H_0W(y,z)$. If $$\\int_\\Omega\\left[\n \\partial_pL_g(y,u,Du)\\cdot D\\zeta\n +\\mathcal B(y,u,Du)\\zeta\\right]dy=0\n \\qquad(\\zeta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega)),$$ then $u\\in C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-337.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-equality-graph-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be open, with $m\\ge1$, and let $u\\in C^{1,\\alpha}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ for $0<\\alpha<1$. Let $G(y,z)$ be the matrix of a smooth Riemannian metric on a neighborhood of the graph of $u$ in $\\mathbb R^m\\times\\mathbb R$, and put $$W(y,z)=\\sqrt{\\det G(y,z)},\\qquad\n L_g(y,z,p)=W(y,z)\n \\left[(-p,1)^TG(y,z)^{-1}(-p,1)\\right]^{1/2}.$$ Fix $H_0>0$ and write $\\mathcal B(y,z,p)=\\partial_zL_g(y,z,p)-H_0W(y,z)$. If $$\\int_\\Omega\\left[\n \\partial_pL_g(y,u,Du)\\cdot D\\zeta\n +\\mathcal B(y,u,Du)\\zeta\\right]dy=0\n \\qquad(\\zeta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega)),$$ then $u\\in C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\Omega\\subset\\mathbb R^m$ be open, with $m\\ge1$, and let $u\\in C^{1,\\alpha}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$ for $0<\\alpha<1$. Let $G(y,z)$ be the matrix of a smooth Riemannian metric on a neighborhood of the graph of $u$ in $\\mathbb R^m\\times\\mathbb R$, and put $$W(y,z)=\\sqrt{\\det G(y,z)},\\qquad\n L_g(y,z,p)=W(y,z)\n \\left[(-p,1)^TG(y,z)^{-1}(-p,1)\\right]^{1/2}.$$ Fix $H_0>0$ and write $\\mathcal B(y,z,p)=\\partial_zL_g(y,z,p)-H_0W(y,z)$. If $$\\int_\\Omega\\left[\n \\partial_pL_g(y,u,Du)\\cdot D\\zeta\n +\\mathcal B(y,u,Du)\\zeta\\right]dy=0\n \\qquad(\\zeta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega)),$$ then $u\\in C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$.",
      "explanation": "Upgrades a stationary graph from Hölder gradient regularity to a locally Lipschitz gradient.",

          "source_id": "PM-337.1"
        }
      ],

      "quantifiers": [

If $$\\int_\\Omega\\left[\n \\partial_pL_g(y,u,Du)\\cdot D\\zeta\n +\\mathcal B(y,u,Du)\\zeta\\right]dy=0\n \\qquad(\\zeta\\in C_c^\\infty(\\Omega)),$$ then $u\\in C^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\Omega)$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Cartan–Hadamard manifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *Cartan–Hadamard manifold* is a complete simply connected smooth Riemannian manifold with nonpositive sectional curvature.
- **Model isoperimetric profile** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The model isoperimetric profile is $$\mathcal I_b(V)=\mathcal A_b\bigl(\mathcal V_b^{-1}(V)\bigr), \qquad V\ge0.$$
- **Ambient perimeter** ([source](#eq:ambient-perimeter)): For a measurable set $E\subset M$, its *ambient perimeter* is $$\label{eq:ambient-perimeter} P_g(E)=\|D\chi_E\|_g(M) =\sup\left\{\int_E\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_g X\,d\mathop{\mathrm{Vol}}_g: X\in C_c^\infty(TM),\ \|X\|_g\le1\right\}.$$
- **Confined isoperimetric profile** ([source](#eq:confined-profile)): Define $$\label{eq:confined-profile} I(v)=\inf\bigl\{P(E):\ \|E\setminus B\|=0,\ \|E\|=v\bigr\}, \qquad 0<v<\|B\|.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-balancing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-balancing): Fixed-point balancing -> Balanced map
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-thm-extrinsic-sobolev) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subthreshold-minimizer-2): Subthreshold compactness and balanced variations -> Sharp hypersurface Sobolev inequality
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-confined-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-confined-regularity): BV compactness and obstacle regularity -> Confined minimizer
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-lem-constant-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-constant-test): Area growth and capacity cutoff passage -> Legitimate constant test
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-profile-ode) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-profile-ode): Mean-curvature bound inserted into the constant test -> Profile differential inequality
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-thm-normalized-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-normalized-comparison): Integration of the inverse model profile -> Bounded-set model comparison
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-volume-cutoff-2): Classical lower-dimensional comparison, BV cutoff, and metric scaling -> Full finite-volume comparison
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-thm-equality) / [proof](#sec:rigidity): Boundary localization, phase recovery, and metric domination -> Whole-region Euclidean rigidity
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-cor-model-ball-spectral) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-equality-cutoffs-2): Equimeasurable radial rearrangement -> Spectral and torsional comparisons

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-perimeter**: Full ambient perimeter is bounded below by the equal-volume model-ball perimeter.
- **I-rigidity**: Equality in the Euclidean perimeter comparison is equivalent to agreement up to ambient volume zero with an open region whose induced metric is isometric to a round Euclidean ball.
- **I-sobolev**: The hypersurface energy bounds the critical norm with the sharp Euclidean constant.
- **I-spectral**: At equal volume, the model ball bounds the first Dirichlet variational value from below and the quotient-normalized torsional rigidity from above.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-geometry**: Apply the comparison directly to a discrete graph or sampled surface. -> The theorem supplies no transfer from discrete data to smooth ambient curvature and perimeter.
- **A-contact**: Replace ambient perimeter by free boundary area inside the confining ball. -> Omitting contact boundary changes the perimeter minimized in the proof.
- **A-rigidity**: Infer that the entire ambient manifold is flat from equality. -> The equality conclusion determines the induced metric only on the equality region.
- **A-constant**: Insert the constant test solely because the perimeter support is compact. -> The regular part can be noncompact after its singular set is removed.
- **A-balance**: Treat the balancing existence proof as a terminating solver for sampled measures. -> The measure must be nonatomic, and the fixed-point argument supplies no finite solver contract.
- **A-normalization**: Transfer the torsional comparison to an unspecified torsion normalization. -> Changing the defining quotient changes the quantity being compared.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The source proves continuum perimeter and rigidity comparisons for smooth Riemannian manifolds. Its balancing argument uses Brouwer fixed-point existence, and its constant-test argument passes through cutoffs with vanishing Dirichlet energy. These do not provide a finite constructive input/output contract or an effective termination bound. Evaluating model-ball formulas alone would not decide the asserted comparisons for arbitrary measurable sets.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Perimeter comparison requires a complete simply connected smooth Riemannian manifold with the stated nonpositive upper sectional curvature bound. Blocked use: ['Apply only after establishing the manifold and sectional curvature hypotheses.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): The comparison requires finite volume and finite full ambient perimeter. Unbounded finite-volume sets remain within its scope. Blocked use: ['Do not extend the theorem to infinite volume or infinite ambient perimeter.', 'Do not reject a finite-volume set merely because it is unbounded.']
- **L3** (edge_case): Full ambient perimeter includes boundary on a confining sphere; relative perimeter inside the confining region cannot replace it. Blocked use: ['Do not discard contact boundary when comparing perimeter.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): Euclidean equality rigidity requires a bounded measurable set of positive finite volume and finite ambient perimeter. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer equality classification to unbounded or null-volume sets from the comparison theorem.']
- **L5** (edge_case): Equality determines the induced metric on the whole equality region and does not require flatness outside that region. Blocked use: ['Do not infer global ambient flatness from equality.', 'Do not substitute boundary containment for whole-region isometry.']
- **L6** (hypothesis): The spectral and torsional comparisons use a nonempty smooth relatively compact domain, the stated Riemannian hypotheses and the stated range of the variational exponent. Blocked use: ['Do not transfer these comparisons to arbitrary nonsmooth domains or other exponent regimes without a separate argument.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): The hypersurface critical Sobolev argument uses the stated boundary dimension range and locally regular immersed hypersurfaces. Blocked use: ['Do not evaluate its critical coefficients outside their stated dimension range.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The hypersurface Sobolev inequality is stated for Lipschitz tests compactly supported in the interior and for the stated Dirichlet zero extensions. Blocked use: ['Do not assume arbitrary tests at a boundary or at infinity satisfy the inequality.']
- **L9** (approximation): Compactness of the perimeter support alone does not justify the constant test on the regular locus; singular cutoffs with vanishing Dirichlet energy are required. Blocked use: ['Do not discard singular-set capacity and area-growth hypotheses when passing to the constant test.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The balancing result requires a compactly supported nonatomic Borel probability measure. Blocked use: ['Do not substitute an atomic sampled measure for the stated measure hypothesis.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): Subthreshold variational attainment supplies existence; a finite representation, terminating minimizer algorithm and runtime bound are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not infer a finite algorithm or complexity guarantee from attainment of the variational infimum.']
- **L12** (counterexample_scope): The comparison is a smooth Riemannian result. Transfer certificates for discrete graphs, meshes or sampled models are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Do not treat a discrete or sampled representation as satisfying the geometric hypotheses without a proved transfer.']
- **L13** (hypothesis): The confined profile derivative bound holds almost everywhere in the open volume interval. Blocked use: ['Do not assert a classical derivative at every volume or at the interval endpoints.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the generalized Cartan–Hadamard isoperimetric conjecture in every dimension. In a complete simply connected smooth manifold with sectional curvature at most <i>κ</i> ≤ 0, every finite-volume set of finite ambient perimeter has perimeter at least that of the equal-volume ball in curvature <i>κ</i>. For bounded positive-volume sets, equality in the Euclidean comparison holds precisely when the set agrees up to null sets with an open region isometric, with its induced metric, to a round Euclidean ball.
